neighbor’s rental paperwork, all at once, even though I was already overwhelmed and couldn’t keep up. \n- S041 (Painful_Consequence_Behavior): In one of those 'up' periods I almost put a big chunk of But looking back, they seem to last for at least a few days to maybe a week or so before I swing back down.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt so wound up and active, did it ever cause major disruptions in your life, lead to hospitalization, or make others feel you had lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: No, I've never been hospitalized or lost touch with reality, but those periods definitely caused some disruptions in my life. I would take on far too many commitments that I couldn't keep up with, and I even almost put a large chunk of our savings into an eco-housing project before my daughter talked me out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a high-energy period that lasted a full week or longer and completely prevented you from working or managing your daily life?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, those wound-up periods didn't stop me from functioning; if anything, I felt like I had *too* much energy and was taking on way too much at once. It's actually when I sink back down into these dark, heavy depressions that I completely shut down, back out of work, and can't manage my daily life.

But looking back, they seem to last for at least a few days to maybe a week or so before I swing back down.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt so wound up and active, did it ever cause major disruptions in your life, lead to hospitalization, or make others feel you had lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: No, I've never been hospitalized or lost touch with reality, but those periods definitely caused some disruptions in my life. I would take on far too many commitments that I couldn't keep up with, and I even almost put a large chunk of our savings into an eco-housing project before my daughter talked me out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a high-energy period that lasted a full week or longer and completely prevented you from working or managing your daily life?\n\nPatient: No, those wound-up periods didn't stop me from functioning; if anything, I felt like I had *too* much energy and was taking on way too much at once. It's actually when I sink back down into these dark, heavy depressions that I completely shut down, back out of work, and can't manage my daily life.\n\nDoctor: During any of your high-energy periods, did you ever have grandiose beliefs about yourself, like having special powers, or engage in highly risky behaviors that were completely out of character?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those wound-up spells I'd feel like I was the only one who could truly fix how our condo community runs, like an expert who saw things nobody else could. I also almost invested a large portion of our savings into an online eco-housing project because I thought I knew better than our financial advisor, but luckily my daughter talked me out of it."

{"candidates": ["F31.4", "F31.8"], "note": "The patient describes recurrent episodes of depression and high-energy states. The high-energy states last a few days to a week, are characterized by racing thoughts, decreased need for sleep, grandiosity, and mild impulsivity, but do not cause severe functional impairment or hospitalization, which is highly suggestive of hypomania rather than mania. This points towards Bipolar II disorder (F31.8) rather than Bipolar I (where a current severe depressive episode would be coded as F31.4). I will keep both candidates for now and ask about substance use and general medical history to rule out secondary causes of these mood swings.", "is_final": false}

Looking back, these swings between feeling wound-up and then sinking into these dark depressions have been coming and going for at least a year, but they've really ramped up lately.\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me more about those times you felt \"wound-up\"—did you experience a decreased need for sleep, racing thoughts, or feel unusually active and full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those times I felt incredibly wired and touchy, and my thoughts would race so fast that it was hard to shut my brain off to sleep. I’d have this sudden burst of energy where I'd be up before dawn cleaning closets, reorganizing files, and taking on multiple volunteer projects all at once because I just couldn't sit still.\n\nDoctor: When you have these high-energy, \"wound-up\" periods, how long do they usually last at a time—is it just a few days, or does it go on for a week or more?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number of days, because I tend to lose track of time when my mind is racing so fast. But looking back, they seem to last for at least a few days to maybe a week or so before I swing back down.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt so wound up and active, did it ever cause major disruptions in your life, lead to hospitalization, or make others feel you had lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: No, I've never been hospitalized or lost touch with reality, but those periods definitely caused some disruptions in my life. I would take on far too many commitments that I couldn't keep up with, and I even almost put a large chunk of our savings into an eco-housing project before my daughter talked me out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a high-energy period that lasted a full week or longer and completely prevented you from working or managing your daily life?\n\nPatient: No, those wound-up periods didn't stop me from functioning; if anything, I felt like I had *too* much energy and was taking on way too much at once. It's actually when I sink back down into these dark, heavy depressions that I completely shut down, back out of work, and can't manage my daily life.\n\nDoctor: During any of your high-energy periods, did you ever have grandiose beliefs about yourself, like having special powers, or engage in highly risky behaviors that were completely out of character?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those wound-up spells I'd feel like I was the only one who could truly fix how our condo community runs, like an expert who saw things nobody else could. I also almost invested a large portion of our savings into an online eco-housing project because I thought I knew better than our financial advisor, but luckily my daughter talked me out of it.\n\nDoctor: To be sure, have you ever had a high-energy period that lasted a full week or longer where you felt completely out of control, or did things that put your safety or others' safety at serious risk?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had periods of high energy, like a stretch a few weeks ago for several days where I was up cleaning before dawn and my thoughts were racing. However, I never felt completely out of control or did anything that put our physical safety at risk. The closest I got was almost making that reckless decision with our savings, but my daughter stepped in to talk me down before I could do it.\n\nDoctor: When you have these high-energy, \"wound-up\" periods, do they typically last for at least four days in a row, or are they usually shorter than that?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure of the exact number of days those periods last. I haven't really tracked them precisely, especially since my mind is usually racing so much when they happen."

I’d have this sudden burst of energy where I'd be up before dawn cleaning closets, reorganizing files, and taking on multiple volunteer projects all at once because I just couldn't sit still.\n\nDoctor: When you have these high-energy, \"wound-up\" periods, how long do they usually last at a time—is it just a few days, or does it go on for a week or more?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number of days, because I tend to lose track of time when my mind is racing so fast. But looking back, they seem to last for at least a few days to maybe a week or so before I swing back down.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt so wound up and active, did it ever cause major disruptions in your life, lead to hospitalization, or make others feel you had lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: No, I've never been hospitalized or lost touch with reality, but those periods definitely caused some disruptions in my life. I would take on far too many commitments that I couldn't keep up with, and I even almost put a large chunk of our savings into an eco-housing project before my daughter talked me out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a high-energy period that lasted a full week or longer and completely prevented you from working or managing your daily life?\n\nPatient: No, those wound-up periods didn't stop me from functioning; if anything, I felt like I had *too* much energy and was taking on way too much at once. It's actually when I sink back down into these dark, heavy depressions that I completely shut down, back out of work, and can't manage my daily life.\n\nDoctor: During any of your high-energy periods, did you ever have grandiose beliefs about yourself, like having special powers, or engage in highly risky behaviors that were completely out of character?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those wound-up spells I'd feel like I was the only one who could truly fix how our condo community runs, like an expert who saw things nobody else could. I also almost invested a large portion of our savings into an online eco-housing project because I thought I knew better than our financial advisor, but luckily my daughter talked me out of it.\n\nDoctor: To be sure, have you ever had a high-energy period that lasted a full week or longer where you felt completely out of control, or did things that put your safety or others' safety at serious risk?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had periods of high energy, like a stretch a few weeks ago for several days where I was up cleaning before dawn and my thoughts were racing. However, I never felt completely out of control or did anything that put our physical safety at risk. The closest I got was almost making that reckless decision with our savings, but my daughter stepped in to talk me down before I could do it.\n\nDoctor: When you have these high-energy, \"wound-up\" periods, do they typically last for at least four days in a row, or are they usually shorter than that?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure of the exact number of days those periods last. I haven't really tracked them precisely, especially since my mind is usually racing so much when they happen.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your daughter or other people close to you mention that you seemed like a completely different person, and did they notice this change lasting for at least several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Yes, my husband and daughter definitely noticed those sharp swings and could tell I was completely different from my usual self. During those wound-up phases, like when I was talking a mile a minute and starting endless projects a few weeks ago, they could easily see the change. They said these high-energy periods would go on for days or even weeks at a time."

Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick). Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt. Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.
